<!-- Audit 2026-07-02, finding R1-20 (upload_dir CWD-relative; the task brief labeled it R1-19). -->

## Summary
`security.upload_dir` defaulted to the CWD-relative `output/uploads` and was **never passed through the P0a app-home resolver** — only `output.output_dir` was resolved (`backend/main.py:1992`). So the packaged `indexer --app` (which `scripts/install.py` advertises as launchable from any shell) wrote browser uploads into whatever CWD the shell happened to have — or 500'd on an unwritable one — while the SQLite DB and reels correctly lived under `RALLY_APP_HOME`. Exactly the CWD-vs-app-home class of bug P0a was built to eliminate.

**Fix:** `backend/api/uploads.py::_upload_cfg` now resolves `upload_dir` through the same `resolve_output_dir` (`backend/utils/app_home.py`) used for `output.output_dir`, mirroring its semantics exactly:
- `RALLY_APP_HOME` **unset** → the configured v
############################## PR #416 ##############################
title   : fix(storage): gdrive-api — chunked streaming download + loss-proof idempotent put
branch  : fix/gdrive-api-chunked-download-safe-put -> master
created : 2026-07-02T04:53:18Z  updated: 2026-07-02T04:53:18Z
merge   : MERGEABLE CLEAN
size    : +199/-21 across 2 files, 1 commits
CI      : {'SUCCESS': 11} (11 checks)
--- body (first 900 chars) ---
## What / why

Fixes audit finding **R1-18** (AUDIT_REPORT_khelsutra-guru_2026-07-02) on `backend/storage/gdrive_api.py` — two latent hazards on the Drive-API storage hot path before the reel round-trip goes live:

1. **Whole-file RAM buffering on download.** `fetch_to_local` did `get_media().execute()`, materializing the entire object as one `bytes` value — an OOM waiting to happen for a multi-GB source/reel on a memory-limited Cloud Run box. It now streams via `googleapiclient.http.MediaIoBaseDownload` in bounded **32 MiB chunks** (lazy import behind a `_downloader` seam, mirroring the existing `_media_body` seam/convention). The stream writes to a `dst + ".part"` sibling and `os.replace`s into place, so a mid-download failure can never leave a truncated `dst` that a later `overwrite=False` call would trust as complete.

2. **Delete-before-upload data-loss window in `put()`.** A re-put

--- body (first 900 chars) ---
Fixes audit finding **R1-21** (AUDIT_REPORT_khelsutra-guru_2026-07-02): a bucket-poll timeout abandons a still-running L4 execution.

## Summary

**What:** `CloudRunCompute.run_infer` blocked on `poll_until(...)`; when the poll deadline (`max_wait_sec`, default 1800s) hit, `PolicyTimeout` propagated straight to `main.py`'s dispatch handler, which marked the local job terminal-failed. Nothing cancelled the still-running Cloud Run execution (it kept billing the L4 until the job's `--task-timeout 3600`), and because the poll budget undercuts the task budget, a slow-but-healthy 30-60 min inference *systematically* ended as a local "cloud dispatch error" while its late done-marker + outputs were silently wasted.

**How (on `PolicyTimeout`):**
- **ONE final done-marker check** — a worker that finished just past the poll deadline is rescued: its completed result is ingested normally (no cancel,
############################## PR #418 ##############################
title   : fix(api): /api/source downloads a remote source ONCE into a reusable cache (R1-03)
branch  : fix/api-source-remote-cache -> master
created : 2026-07-02T04:55:50Z  updated: 2026-07-02T04:55:50Z
merge   : CONFLICTING DIRTY
size    : +225/-9 across 2 files, 1 commits
CI      : {'SUCCESS': 11} (11 checks)
--- body (first 900 chars) ---
## What

Fixes audit finding **R1-03**: `GET /api/source/{video_id}` called `storage.acquire_local_input` on **every** request, and for a REMOTE-backed video (`gs://` / `s3://` / `gdrive://`…) that meant a fresh `tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="rally-input-")` + a **full object download per request** — never deleted. `FileResponse` serves Range, so one browser scrubbing a `<video>` tag issues many independent requests to the same URL: ~10 seeks on a 4 GB match ≈ ~40 GB of bucket egress **and** ~40 GB of orphaned temp dirs, with every seek stalling behind a multi-GB download.

Now (`backend/main.py::_acquire_source_for_serving`):

- A remote source is fetched **once** into `<output>/source_cache/<video_id>/<name>` and every later request streams that local copy.
- **Atomic publish**: the download goes to a unique `.<name>.<uuid>.partial` in the cache dir, then `os.replace` onto the served name —
############################## PR #419 ##############################
title   : fix(main): --output-dir argparse default no longer clobbers config.json output.output_dir
branch  : fix/output-dir-config-clobber -> master
created : 2026-07-02T04:57:15Z  updated: 2026-07-02T04:57:15Z
merge   : CONFLICTING DIRTY
size    : +225/-12 across 4 files, 1 commits
CI      : {'SUCCESS': 11} (11 checks)
--- body (first 900 chars) ---
## What

`--output-dir` had argparse default `"output"` and was applied **unconditionally** at `main()` init (`backend/main.py`), so a config-set `output.output_dir` (the documented knob, `backend/config/models.py::OutputConfig`) could never take effect on the main entry path. The SQLite DB path derives from it, so the server silently created a fresh empty DB under `./output` while `run_eval.py`/`run_regression.py` (via `backend.config.default_db_path`) resolved the DB from config.json — split state / perceived data loss. (Audit finding R1-19, 2026-07-02.)

## How

- Argparse default is now `None` (= "not passed").
- New pure helper `backend/utils/paths.py::resolve_startup_output_dir(cli_output_dir, config)`: an **explicit** CLI `--output-dir` wins; otherwise config.json's `output.output_dir` is honored via the existing `output_base()`; **P0a app-home rooting** (`resolve_output_dir`) app

11	## 0. TL;DR

This doc is the execution board for bringing the five-repo workspace back to a healthy state without mixing unrelated changes: first a docs-only tracking PR, then small wave PRs, each branched from the remote default branch, verified against that repo's CI contract, and merged only when green.

87	## 4. Execution design
88	
89	### Wave model
90	
91	Each wave is a theme for scheduling; each row in section 7 is an independent PR. A later wave can begin only when it does not conflict with active uncommitted work in the same repo.
92	
93	- **Wave 0 — tracking and truth sync:** this docs-only PR.
94	- **Wave 1 — engine cost/correctness:** paid re-bill, remote-source egress, ffmpeg timeouts, failed mypy fix.
95	- **Wave 2 — engine resource durability:** temp scratch lifecycle, GDrive API streaming/safe put, Cloud Run timeout handling, upload retention scheduling.
96	- **Wave 3 — supply-chain and CI gates:** SHA pins, lock/constraints plan, obsreport CI, sports-data-collector ruff/coverage, khelsutra E2E CUJ, workflow dedupe.
97	- **Wave 4 — UX/UI resilience:** vendored SPA bundle, job resume/progress, upload filename, demo-grid hiding, HTML 5xx handling, WhatsApp file share. This wave carries the screencast requirement.
98	- **Wave 5 — structural debt:** close/re-cut `#390`, continue `main.py` split, then shim/cycle cleanup and database split only after behavior fixes settle.
99	- **Wave 6 — issue hygiene and archival:** close/update stale issues, archive terminal docs only after due diligence.
100	
101	### Verification policy
102	
103	For every code PR:
104	
105	- Run the repo's normal CI-equivalent checks locally when feasible, or cite the green CI rollup and run only local checks that CI cannot cover, matching the repo's review policy.
106	- Preserve or raise coverage floors. Never lower a coverage floor without owner approval.
107	- Install missing test dependencies if the suite otherwise fails to collect; record unavoidable private/GPU/real-video skips.
108	- For engine PRs, default target is `python run_all_tests.py --cov=backend --cov=training --cov-report=term --cov-fail-under=70`, `ruff check .`, and `mypy backend training`, adjusted only for documented CI-policy exceptions.
109	- For `khelsutra/web` PRs, run typecheck, coverage, build, and any relevant Playwright CUJ; create/validate screencast evidence for UI behavior.
110	- For visual/UI PRs, attach the screencast or screenshots in the PR body and store durable artifacts under the existing `khelsutra-screencast/` workflow when the repo's harness supports it.
111	
112	## 5. Risk table
113	
114	| Risk | Where it bites | Mitigation |
115	|---|---|---|
116	| Shared checkout absorbs sibling changes | All repos | Fetch/branch from `origin/<base>`, re-verify branch + HEAD before staging, stage explicit paths. |
117	| Docs-only PR accidentally includes local WIP | Especially collector `.gitignore` | First PR touches engine docs only; later collector PR stages explicit paths and handles `.gitignore` deliberately. |
118	| Engine behavior fixes conflict with stale `#390` | `backend/main.py` | Land correctness/resource fixes first, then close/re-cut the split from fresh `origin/master`. |
119	| UI fixes regress layout or localized text | `khelsutra/web`, `site`, extension UI | Unit/e2e coverage plus Playwright screenshots/screencast validation. |
120	| Local cleanup looks durable but is machine-only | untracked debris / `.git` gc | Record local-only as local-only; add durable ignore/cleanup tests only when needed. |
121	| Owner-gated work gets blended into engineering PRs | Cloudflare, corpus, legal, launch | Keep gate rows marked `Gated? Owner`; PRs may document but not perform. |
122	
123	## 6. Honest limits
124	
125	A green docs-only PR proves only that the project is tracked and links are valid. It does not fix runtime debt.
126	
127	A green unit suite does not prove GPU, WSL, real-video, Cloudflare, Google Drive desktop, or GCS bucket behavior unless the PR explicitly runs those paths or records owner verification.
128	
129	Screencasts prove rendered behavior for the captured viewport and flow; they do not replace automated tests. Any UI PR that fixes a bug must still add or update deterministic tests where feasible.
130

175	## 9. Definition of done
176	
177	- [ ] All non-owner-gated P0/P1/P2/P3 audit findings either resolved, explicitly reclassified, or moved into a narrower tracked project.

180	- [ ] Engine vendored SPA bundle is current or has a durable staleness signal.
181	- [ ] SPA processing UX survives refresh and gives testers visible job/file state.
182	- [ ] UI-visible PRs include validated visual artifacts and deterministic tests where feasible.
183	- [ ] Stale deployment docs/issues no longer direct future sessions at retired infrastructure.
184	- [ ] Owner-gated items remain clearly labeled and have no accidental code/data mutation.
185	- [ ] This doc's section 7 rows are updated with merged PR numbers.
186	- [ ] `Status` flips to `DONE`, inbound references are updated, and the doc is archived per `DOC_STATUS.md` section 2.

(2) `main.py` size "~1300" → "~1450" (now 1448 lines) in §3b/§6/§8.1.
 - `2026-06-25` — **P17 done (B2 finish).** The 4 callers that dropped provider error metrics (`trajectory_hybrid.py`, `yolo_hybrid.py`, `gemini_refine.refine_window`, `gemini_refine.full_video_rallies`) now check `metrics["error"]` and log loudly instead of silently treating a provider failure as "0 rallies" — matching the pattern the `gemini` segmenter already uses. (`yolo_hybrid` was caught during PR #378 review — it bound `metrics` but never read it.) Suite 1484/1484 green; ruff + mypy clean. Follow-up: migrate the hybrids' `process_video` return type from bare `List[Dict]` to the ABC `Result[...]` contract (motion.py is already migrated; hybrids suppressed with `type: ignore[override]` for now).
 - `2026-06-25` — **P9 done (B1 finish).** Removed fabricated `random` data from the motion segmenter — server/receiver/shot_count/avg_speed_kmh → `None`, serve/smash/foul/winner events dropped entirely (motion was the only `add_event` caller). PR #385 review caught a real regression: `run_process_and_stitch.py` crashed on `None >= min_shot_count` (the `.get(…, 0)` default no longer applied) — fixed with a None-guard matching the API path. Suite 1492/1492 green; ruff + mypy clean.
+- `2026-07-02` — **P10 currency refresh (docs-only, audit-reconcile).** `backend/main.py` re-measured: **2198 lines** (was 1448 on 2026-06-25 — 10 PRs landed on it since, incl. #400/#405–#409/#412/#413); sizes refreshed in §3b/§6/§8.1. Header status/tracking-anchor also corrected (Phase-2 progress; the tracker is **§6**, not §7). No code change.
 - `2026-06-26` — **P12 CI fix (#386).** The D13/P12 PR failed the `full-suite` lane with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'trackers'`. Two root causes: (1) the two new tests hard-imported the standalone `trackers` package, but it's a git-only dep (PyPI wheel is metadata-only — roboflow/trackers#474) deliberately omitted from `pyproject.toml`, and the lane installs via `pip install -e .[dev]`; (2) the git ref `@v2.5.0` was wrong — roboflow/trackers uses bare version tags (`2.5.0`), so pip failed with `pathspec 'v2.5.0' did not match`. Fixes: added a `pip install trackers @ git+…@2.5.0` step to both `ci.yml` and `nightly.yml` full-suite lanes (same out-of-band pattern as torch); fixed the tag in `requirements.txt`; and guarded the package-importing test with `pytest.importorskip("trackers")` (matching the repo's `obsreport` soft-dep convention). All 10 CI checks green.

Verified against `master@d00b4db` that both R1-18 hazards are still present and **not** superseded by any of #422–#428 (none touched `backend/storage/gdrive_api.py`):
- `gdrive_api.py:144` still whole-file buffers the download (`get_media().execute()` → one `bytes`);
- `put()` still **deletes-before-create** (delete at :165 → create at :166) — the data-loss window.

The fix is correct and self-contained: chunked `MediaIoBaseDownload` → `dst + ".part"` → `os.replace` (no truncated `dst` survives a mid-download failure), and `files().update()` on re-put so the old object survives a failed upload while preserving the #338 idempotent-put contract. 4 new offline tests.

- `2026-06-25` — **P17 done (B2 finish).** The 4 callers that dropped provider error metrics (`trajectory_hybrid.py`, `yolo_hybrid.py`, `gemini_refine.refine_window`, `gemini_refine.full_video_rallies`) now check `metrics["error"]` and log loudly instead of silently treating a provider failure as "0 rallies" — matching the pattern the `gemini` segmenter already uses. (`yolo_hybrid` was caught during PR #378 review — it bound `metrics` but never read it.) Suite 1484/1484 green; ruff + mypy clean. Follow-up: migrate the hybrids' `process_video` return type from bare `List[Dict]` to the ABC `Result[...]` contract (motion.py is already migrated; hybrids suppressed with `type: ignore[override]` for now).
- `2026-06-25` — **P9 done (B1 finish).** Removed fabricated `random` data from the motion segmenter — server/receiver/shot_count/avg_speed_kmh → `None`, serve/smash/foul/winner events dropped entirely (motion was the only `add_event` caller). PR #385 review caught a real regression: `run_process_and_stitch.py` crashed on `None >= min_shot_count` (the `.get(…, 0)` default no longer applied) — fixed with a None-guard matching the API path. Suite 1492/1492 green; ruff + mypy clean.
- `2026-06-26` — **P12 CI fix (#386).** The D13/P12 PR failed the `full-suite` lane with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'trackers'`. Two root causes: (1) the two new tests hard-imported the standalone `trackers` package, but it's a git-only dep (PyPI wheel is metadata-only — roboflow/trackers#474) deliberately omitted from `pyproject.toml`, and the lane installs via `pip install -e .[dev]`; (2) the git ref `@v2.5.0` was wrong — roboflow/trackers uses bare version tags (`2.5.0`), so pip failed with `pathspec 'v2.5.0' did not match`. Fixes: added a `pip install trackers @ git+…@2.5.0` step to both `ci.yml` and `nightly.yml` full-suite lanes (same out-of-band pattern as torch); fixed the tag in `requirements.txt`; and guarded the package-importing test with `pytest.importorskip("trackers")` (matching the repo's `obsreport` soft-dep convention). All 10 CI checks green.

115	## 5. The gap — what's still local-only (why the box is still a blocker)
116	
117	All of this is **tiny except the originals** — the metadata that blocks cloud eval is < 25 MB total:
118	
119	| Data | Where it is now | Size | In bucket? | Needed for |
120	|---|---|---|---|---|
121	| 20 WASB trajectory CSVs | `C:\…\Annotation Setup\Collect\Trajectories\` | **17.5 MB** | ❌ none | eval/litmus (cached). *(No `cached` train source exists — train synths or detector-extracts.)* |
122	| golden manifest | `output/human_lovo_manifest.json` (C:-absolute paths) | 4.5 KB | ❌ | eval corpus definition |
123	| original-6 labels | `F:\[Khelsutra]…\Training\Golden Labelled\*.rallies.csv` | ~12 KB | ❌ | train + eval |
124	| 7 new-clip labels | repo `output/*.rallies.csv` (gitignored) **+** F: archive (backed up 2026-06-22) | ~12 KB | ⚠ partial/inconsistent | train + eval |
125	| source videos / proxies | F: archive + Google Drive golden folder | **100s of GB** | ⚠ 3 only | infer + train (real WASB extraction) |
126	
127	> The corpus *metadata* (trajectories + labels + manifest, ~25 MB) is what actually blocks GPU-free
128	> cloud eval and the served Gate-A litmus — and it's trivially small to migrate. The **videos** are the
129	> only large migration, and they already exist on **Google Drive** (the golden-corpus folder), so they
130	> can go **Drive → bucket from a cloud box**, keeping the local machine out of the path entirely.
131	
132	---
133	
134	## 6. Migration plan (close the gap)
135	
136	**Phase 1 — corpus metadata (≈25 MB, do first, removes the eval blocker).** Stage all trajectories,
137	all labels, and a **bucket-relative eval manifest** into `gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus`. Pending the §7
138	naming decision. Sketch:
139	
140	```bash
141	# trajectories (decision-free; absent today)
142	gcloud storage cp "C:\…\Collect\Trajectories\*.csv" gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/trajectories/
143	# labels (after the §7.1 naming decision) -> labels/<canonical-name>.rallies.csv
144	# eval manifest with gs:// (or bucket-relative) paths -> a stable manifest object (§7.3)
145	```
146	
147	**Phase 2 — videos → `videos/<name>.mp4`** (bucket name = the eval `name`, consistent with
148	`trajectories/` + `labels/`). Sources (resolved 2026-06-22 — see `scratch/copy_videos_to_gcs.ps1`):
149	
150	| Source | Clips | Size |
151	|---|---|---|
152	| Drive golden folder `…/Golden Label Videos Request - June 15/[Done] Video N/` (**proxies**) | `mahadevpura_1/2`, `GX010128`, `GX020094`, `GX030094`, `Badminton_BXH_2`, `Boxhill_Doubles` (7) | ~13.5 GB |
153	| F: archive (**full MP4s** — proxy-first before upload) | `GX010137`, `GX010141`, + original-6 (`adarsh_avi_singles`, `kushagra_singles`, `largetest_doubles`, `gbaaddy`, `testlarge_short`, `mahadevpura_singles`) (8) | ~50 GB |
154	
155	**Two ways to run it:**
156	- **Decoupled (preferred — no local box):** `rclone` on a cloud VM with a Google-Drive remote →
157	  `rclone copy gdrive:"<…/[Done] Video N>/<proxy>.mp4" :gcs:khelsutra-rally-corpus/videos/<name>.mp4`
158	  (one-time owner step: `rclone config` → `gdrive` remote OAuth). Or `gdown <file-id>` on the box for
159	  the shared folder. This is the path that actually removes the local box.
160	- **Interim (today, owner box — streams Drive/F: → local → GCS):** `pwsh scratch/copy_videos_to_gcs.ps1
161	  -Execute` (dry-run without `-Execute`; `-Only <name>` for one; skip-existing). Uses local bandwidth.
162	
163	**Priority:** the 7 Drive **proxies** first (already 1080p, sufficient for detection). The 8 large F:
164	full MP4s should be **proxied to 1080p first** (cheaper storage + faster cloud decode) before upload.
165	⚠ Two pre-existing non-canonical video objects (`videos/GX030094_proxy.mp4`, `videos/mahadevpura-1.MP4`)
166	are superseded once `videos/GX030094.mp4` / `videos/mahadevpura_1.mp4` land — verify-dupe-then-delete
167	(same as the label cleanup). Track per-clip in §8.
168	
169	**Phase 3 — make eval/litmus read GCS (real code, NOT a config flip).** The eval drivers
170	(`served_gate_a.evaluate_video`, `calibrate_wasb.load_golden_gts`, `TrackNetRunner.parse_trajectory_csv`)
171	open `spec["trajectory"]`/`spec["labels"]` with plain `open()` and gate on `os.path.isfile()` — they
172	have **no storage-layer fetch**, so a `gs://` manifest cannot be consumed today. Phase-3 must thread
173	`storage.fetch`/`resolve_input_uri` through those paths first. Then: re-probe `fps`/`frame_width` from
174	the bucket videos (don't trust the copied values — a wrong fps silently mis-aligns every label), and
175	**re-measure the served Gate-A litmus on the GCS 15-clip set as a [[launch-report-convention]] event**
176	(dual-set RAW+golden no-regression, not a silent eval-set swap — see PR #236).

179	
180	## 7. Decisions
181	
182	1. **`labels/` naming — LOCKED (owner, 2026-06-22):** `labels/<YYYY-MM-DD>_<name>.rallies.csv`, where
183	   `<name>` is the eval/manifest clip name and `<YYYY-MM-DD>` is the label's **authoring date** =
184	   the *earliest* mtime across all known copies (C: working copy + F: archive — robust to copy-resets;

1	"""Leak-scan guard — refuse to commit obvious PII / attribution leaks into the code surface.
2	
3	A *structural* CI guard (deliberately NOT a name detector): it scans the TRACKED text files under
4	the code/data roots for
5	
6	  (a) a 32-hex MD5 token — the ``video_id`` shape (``backend/utils/hashing.py::compute_video_id``);
7	      16-hex ``fx_`` fixture slugs and 12-hex ``gt_hash`` values do NOT match, by design.
8	  (b) a personal absolute user path — ``C:\\Users\\<name>``, ``/home/<name>/``, ``/Users/<name>/`` —
9	      where ``<name>`` is a real username (documented placeholders like ``<name>``/``<user>`` are OK).
10	
11	Optionally, given a PRIVATE (gitignored) name-list via ``--names`` (default ``<root>/.leakscan_private``
12	if present), it ALSO flags those tokens — so the owner can scan for real player/venue names locally
13	while CI runs only the name-free structural guard (you can't commit a name-list to scan for).
14	
15	A line carrying ``# leakscan-allow`` is skipped (escape hatch for a legitimate token, e.g. an MD5
16	test-vector). Run ``python -m tools.leak_scan`` (exit 1 on any finding). SCOPE = the shipped-code
17	surface (``backend/ training/ scripts/ tools/ eval_baselines/``); ``tests/`` (legit example
18	fixtures), ``docs/``, and gitignored ``scratch/``/``output/`` are NOT scanned (scan those locally
19	with ``--names`` against a private list if desired).
20	"""
21	
22	from __future__ import annotations
23	
24	import argparse
25	import re
26	import subprocess
27	import sys
28	from pathlib import Path
29	from typing import List, Optional, Sequence, Tuple
30	
31	#: Shipped-code + committed-data surfaces scanned. NOT ``tests/`` (fixtures legitimately embed
32	#: example paths/usernames/MD5s — noise for a structural guard; scan those locally with --names),
33	#: and NOT ``docs/``/``scratch/``/``output/`` (intentional operator notes).
34	SCAN_ROOTS: Tuple[str, ...] = (
35	    "backend",
36	    "training",
37	    "scripts",
38	    "tools",
39	    "eval_baselines",
40	)
41	
42	#: This tool + its test legitimately contain sample patterns — never scan them.
43	SELF_EXCLUDE: Tuple[str, ...] = ("tools/leak_scan.py", "tests/test_leak_scan.py")
44	
45	#: A run of EXACTLY 32 hex chars (the MD5 video_id shape). The negative look-around means a 16-hex
46	#: ``fx_`` slug, a 12-hex ``gt_hash``, or a 40/64-hex SHA do NOT match.
47	_MD5_RE = re.compile(r"(?<![0-9a-fA-F])[0-9a-fA-F]{32}(?![0-9a-fA-F])")
48	_WIN_USER_RE = re.compile(r"[A-Za-z]:[\\/]+Users[\\/]+([A-Za-z0-9._-]+)")
49	_NIX_USER_RE = re.compile(r"/(?:home|Users)/([A-Za-z0-9._-]+)/")
50	
51	#: Tokens that are a DOCUMENTED placeholder / generic example, not a real username — so a doc'd
52	#: path like ``C:\\Users\\x`` or ``/home/u/`` does not trip the guard.
53	_PLACEHOLDERS = frozenset(
54	    {
55	        "<name>",
56	        "<user>",
57	        "<username>",
58	        "<you>",
59	        "you",
60	        "youruser",
61	        "yourname",
62	        "user",
63	        "username",
64	        "x",
65	        "u",
66	        "a",
67	        "b",
68	        "c",
69	        "n",
70	        "foo",
71	        "bar",
72	        "baz",
73	        "test",
74	        "testuser",
75	        "tmp",
76	        "home",
77	        "example",
78	    }
79	)
80	
81	_ALLOW = "# leakscan-allow"
82	_TEXT_EXT = {
83	    ".py",
84	    ".json",
85	    ".txt",
86	    ".csv",
87	    ".yml",
88	    ".yaml",
89	    ".cfg",
90	    ".ini",
91	    ".toml",
92	    ".md",
93	}
94	
95	
96	def _tracked_files(root: Path) -> List[str]:
97	    """The tracked files under SCAN_ROOTS (via ``git ls-files`` so only committed paths are scanned)."""
98	    out = subprocess.run(
99	        ["git", "ls-files", *SCAN_ROOTS], cwd=str(root), capture_output=True, text=True
100	    )
101	    return [f.strip() for f in out.stdout.splitlines() if f.strip()]
102	
103	
104	def _load_names(path: Optional[Path]) -> List[str]:
105	    if path is None or not path.is_file():
106	        return []
107	    return [
108	        ln.strip()
109	        for ln in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace").splitlines()
110	        if ln.strip() and not ln.lstrip().startswith("#")
111	    ]
112	
113	
114	def scan_text(text: str, *, names: Sequence[str] = ()) -> List[Tuple[int, str]]:
115	    """Return ``(lineno, reason)`` findings for one file's text (1-based line numbers)."""
116	    findings: List[Tuple[int, str]] = []
117	    lnames = [n.lower() for n in names if n]
118	    for i, line in enumerate(text.splitlines(), start=1):
119	        if _ALLOW in line:
120	            continue
121	        if _MD5_RE.search(line):
122	            findings.append((i, "32-hex token (video_id / MD5 shape)"))
123	        for m in _WIN_USER_RE.finditer(line):
124	            if m.group(1).lower() not in _PLACEHOLDERS:
125	                findings.append(
126	                    (i, f"personal Windows user path (…Users\\{m.group(1)}…)")
127	                )
128	                break
129	        for m in _NIX_USER_RE.finditer(line):
130	            if m.group(1).lower() not in _PLACEHOLDERS:
131	                findings.append((i, f"personal POSIX user path (…/{m.group(1)}/…)"))
132	                break
133	        low = line.lower()
134	        for n in lnames:
135	            if n in low:
136	                findings.append((i, f"private name-list token {n!r}"))
137	                break
138	    return findings
139	
140	
141	def scan_repo(
142	    root: Path, *, names_file: Optional[Path] = None
143	) -> List[Tuple[str, int, str]]:
144	    """Scan the tracked code-surface files under ``root``; return ``(relpath, lineno, reason)``."""
145	    names = _load_names(names_file)
146	    results: List[Tuple[str, int, str]] = []
147	    for rel in _tracked_files(root):
148	        if rel in SELF_EXCLUDE or Path(rel).suffix.lower() not in _TEXT_EXT:
149	            continue
150	        try:
151	            text = (root / rel).read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
152	        except OSError:
153	            continue
154	        for lineno, reason in scan_text(text, names=names):
155	            results.append((rel, lineno, reason))
156	    return results
157	
158	
159	def main(argv: Optional[Sequence[str]] = None) -> int:
160	    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(
161	        description="Leak-scan guard: 32-hex MD5 + personal paths (+ optional private name-list)."
162	    )
163	    ap.add_argument(
164	        "--root",
165	        type=Path,
166	        default=Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1],
167	        help="repo root (default: the parent of tools/)",
168	    )
169	    ap.add_argument(
170	        "--names",
171	        type=Path,
172	        default=None,
173	        help="optional gitignored private name-list (default: <root>/.leakscan_private if present)",
174	    )
175	    args = ap.parse_args(list(argv) if argv is not None else None)
176	    root: Path = args.root
177	    names_file = args.names if args.names is not None else (root / ".leakscan_private")
178	    findings = scan_repo(root, names_file=names_file)
179	    if not findings:
180	        print(
181	            "[leak-scan] clean — no MD5 / personal-path / private-name leaks in the code surface."
182	        )
183	        return 0
184	    print(
185	        f"[leak-scan] {len(findings)} potential leak(s) "
186	        f"(add '# leakscan-allow' on a line to suppress a legitimate one):",
187	        file=sys.stderr,
188	    )
189	    for rel, lineno, reason in findings:
190	        print(f"  {rel}:{lineno}: {reason}", file=sys.stderr)
191	    return 1
192	
193	
194	if __name__ == "__main__":
195	    raise SystemExit(main())
196

4	> **Update cadence:** whenever a doc changes lifecycle state (created / shipped a milestone / delivered / archived), and re-review at each clean-slate.
5	> **Why this exists:** so we can (a) **review completeness** at a glance, (b) **update it as work finishes** to prove we're done, and (c) **never archive a foundational doc without honestly updating it first** (owner directive, 2026-06-21).
6	
7	---
8	
9	## 0. How to use this
10	- **Reviewing now?** Read §4 (the open findings/actions) — that's what needs attention. §3 is the full register.
11	- **Marking something complete?** Update the doc's own **§7 progress tracker** (the source of truth for *its* completeness), then flip its row here, then — if terminal — run the §2 archival checklist.
12	- **This register does NOT duplicate completeness detail** — it indexes lifecycle + archive-readiness and points to each tracked doc's §7.
13	
14	## 1. Lifecycle legend
15	`DRAFT → IN PROGRESS → DONE/DELIVERED → ARCHIVED`. Plus: **LIVING** (permanent reference, never "done"), **PROPOSAL/DESIGN** (owner-gated, not started), **REPORT** (a terminal record of a run/launch), **CONVENTION** (a standing rule/guide).
16	
17	## 2. Archival due-diligence checklist  ⟵ *the repeatable process (do NOT just `git mv`)*
18	Before a foundational/tracked doc moves to `docs/archives/`:
19	1. **VERIFY claims against the code** — no stale assertions; tag load-bearing facts `[verified]`. (A doc that says "nothing built" when code shipped is *not* archive-ready — fix it first.)
20	2. **Flip Status → DELIVERED / DONE / REJECTED + a completion note** — what shipped, where (PRs, paths, artifacts), and what was **deferred / carried forward**.
21	3. **Update ALL inbound references** — `docs/README.md` index, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/NEXT_STEPS.md`, and any cross-doc links → the new path + status.
22	4. **Keep the lineage** — ADR forward/back-links + "supersedes / superseded-by" so the evolution stays traceable.
23	5. **`git mv` the whole dir/file → `docs/archives/past_projects/`** (terminal-state ONLY; live docs stay at `docs/` top level). Relative links inside become historical (note this in the doc's header).
24	6. **Record the move** — flip the row here to §3e + a line in memory `current-state`.
25	
26	> **Worked example (the template):** **DECOUPLED_COMPUTE → #270** — verified M0–M2+M3.5 delivered on GCP, flipped 🚧→✅ DELIVERED with a completion note (incl. carried-forward WASB-C3/DPA/`md5`), reindexed `docs/README` + `past_projects/README`, kept the ADR-010→014 lineage, then `git mv` to `docs/archives/past_projects/DECOUPLED_COMPUTE/`.
27

10	Engine repo is healthy and fast-moving: master at e410136 (2026-07-02), 22 commits merged since 06-26, ~1500 tests, coverage ~85%, CI green on master (trusted). The per-project docs' own §7 trackers are remarkably accurate — every ☑ claim I spot-checked (D13/ByteTrack #386, O1 Gemini GC #380, B1 #385, B2/P17 #378, B3/B6 config fixes) is verifiably true in code. The rot is concentrated in the INDEX layer (DOC_STATUS.md, docs/README.md, BACKLOG.md, NEXT_STEPS.md header), which lags reality by ~15 shipped PRs, and in two designed-but-unstarted regression tracks (M4 quality floor, decision-snapshot). The single biggest live debt: the main.py god-module split (D1/P10) is stalled — PR #390 sits open and CONFLICTING since 06-26 while main.py grew 52% (1448→2198 lines) from 8 new endpoint PRs landing in it.
11	
12	### engine:correctness
13	The engine is a ~2200-line god-module orchestrator (backend/main.py) over a well-factored worker/storage/compute substrate; master is current (HEAD e410136) and CI is trusted green with ~1500 tests. The async job plumbing (claim_due_job CAS + single-worker executor + single-instance DB lock) and the schema ladder (v9, idempotent ALTERs, backup-before-migrate, downgrade guard) are genuinely solid — most crash-safety patterns hold up under scrutiny. The dominant hot-path debt is lifecycle, not logic: paid-segmenter idempotency is still missing (#340 open, confirmed at HEAD), remote-input scratch copies are never reclaimed anywhere (acquire_local_input mkdtemp-per-call, worst on /api/source where every browser Range request re-downloads the full remote video), and the packaged-app path story is inconsistent (upload_dir CWD-relative vs app-home-rooted output_dir; config output.output_dir dead on the CLI path). Cloud-serving has an owner-gated GPU cost leak (poll timeout never cancels the Cloud Run execution). Findings are 2×P1, 5×P2, 2×P3; only #340 is already tracked.
14	
15	### engine:structure
16	Engine repo at master e410136 (current with origin, 2026-07-02). Backend is ~28.9k lines of Python across ~29k total; merge velocity is high (9 PRs touching main.py alone since 06-26). CI is green on master per audit ground rules. The dominant structural debt is the D1 god-module problem: backend/main.py is now 2,198 lines and grew +401 lines (+22%) in the six days since split PR #390 was opened — that PR is now CONFLICTING and rotting. The transitional module-globals shim (extracted API modules re-importing backend.main at call time) creates a 3-module import cycle and keeps the two largest modules mypy-quarantined. Secondary debt: four more >800-line modules, duplicated Stage-2 AI-handoff logic across two live segmenter families, and small hygiene items (7 tracked scratch/ scripts violating the repo's own convention, 4 stale remote branches). All jobs in both workflows carry per-job timeouts, local ruff 0.15.16 passes clean, and the coverage floor (70) is enforced in both the per-PR and nightly full-suite lanes. This doc is the execution board for bringing the five-repo workspace back to a healthy state without mixing unrelated changes: first a docs-only tracking PR, then small wave PRs, each branched from the remote default branch, verified against that repo's CI contract, and merged only when green.
14	
15	Highest-risk remaining themes: paid/egress cost leaks in the engine, temp/scratch lifecycle, one failed mypy fix, stale vendored SPA bundle, missing CI/supply-chain gates in sibling repos, and user-facing SPA processing resilience. Owner-gated work stays flagged, not silently executed.
16	
17	## 1. Problem & goal
18	
19	The audit at `docs/AUDIT_REPORT_khelsutra-guru_2026-07-02.md` confirmed a broad health gap across:
20	
21	- `badminton-highlight-indexer/` — engine correctness, resource lifecycle, supply-chain pinning, CI drift, docs governance.
22	- `khelsutra/` — SPA processing UX, stale engine bundle, stale issue/docs references, E2E coverage gaps.
23	- `sports-data-collector/` — CI maturity, dirty workspace artifact policy, corpus registry integrity.
24	- `rally-annotator/` — browser-extension performance/localization fixes, remaining provenance/i18n-quality issues.
25	- `sports-obsreport/` — no CI and a small customer-facing pluralization defect.
26	- Non-git data dirs — Roora pilot artifacts and screencast evidence need durable handling.
27	
28	Goal: turn the audit into a sequence of reviewable, green PRs that closes the actionable engineering debt, records owner-gated items honestly, and leaves the workspace with current docs, reliable local checks, and no hidden "fresh checkout breaks immediately" traps.
29

110	
111	## 4. Execution design
112	
113	### Wave model
114	
115	Each wave is a theme for scheduling; each row in section 7 is an independent PR. A later wave can begin only when it does not conflict with active uncommitted work in the same repo.
116	
117	- **Wave 0 — tracking and truth sync:** this docs-only PR.
118	- **Wave 1 — engine cost/correctness:** paid re-bill, remote-source egress, ffmpeg timeouts, failed mypy fix.
119	- **Wave 2 — engine resource durability:** temp scratch lifecycle, GDrive API streaming/safe put, Cloud Run timeout handling, upload retention scheduling.
120	- **Wave 3 — supply-chain and CI gates:** SHA pins, lock/constraints plan, obsreport CI, sports-data-collector ruff/coverage, khelsutra E2E CUJ, workflow dedupe.
121	- **Wave 4 — UX/UI resilience:** vendored SPA bundle, job resume/progress, upload filename, demo-grid hiding, HTML 5xx handling, WhatsApp file share. This wave carries the screencast requirement.
122	- **Wave 5 — structural debt:** close/re-cut `#390`, continue `main.py` split, then shim/cycle cleanup and database split only after behavior fixes settle.
123	- **Wave 6 — issue hygiene and archival:** close/update stale issues, archive terminal docs only after due diligence.
124	
125	### Verification policy
126	
127	For every code PR:
128	
129	- Run the repo's normal CI-equivalent checks locally when feasible, or cite the green CI rollup and run only local checks that CI cannot cover, matching the repo's review policy.
130	- Preserve or raise coverage floors. Never lower a coverage floor without owner approval.
131	- Install missing test dependencies if the suite otherwise fails to collect; record unavoidable private/GPU/real-video skips.
132	- For engine PRs, default target is `python run_all_tests.py --cov=backend --cov=training --cov-report=term --cov-fail-under=70`, `ruff check .`, and `mypy backend training`, adjusted only for documented CI-policy exceptions.
133	- For `khelsutra/web` PRs, run typecheck, coverage, build, and any relevant Playwright CUJ; create/validate screencast evidence for UI behavior.
134	- For visual/UI PRs, attach the screencast or screenshots in the PR body and store durable artifacts under the existing `khelsutra-screencast/` workflow when the repo's harness supports it.
135	
136	## 5. Risk table
137	
138	| Risk | Where it bites | Mitigation |
139	|---|---|---|
140	| Shared checkout absorbs sibling changes | All repos | Fetch/branch from `origin/<base>`, re-verify branch + HEAD before staging, stage explicit paths. |
141	| Docs-only PR accidentally includes local WIP | Especially collector `.gitignore` | First PR touches engine docs only; later collector PR stages explicit paths and handles `.gitignore` deliberately. |
142	| Engine behavior fixes conflict with stale `#390` | `backend/main.py` | Land correctness/resource fixes first, then close/re-cut the split from fresh `origin/master`. |
143	| UI fixes regress layout or localized text | `khelsutra/web`, `site`, extension UI | Unit/e2e coverage plus Playwright screenshots/screencast validation. |
144	| Local cleanup looks durable but is machine-only | untracked debris / `.git` gc | Record local-only as local-only; add durable ignore/cleanup tests only when needed. |
145	| Owner-gated work gets blended into engineering PRs | Cloudflare, corpus, legal, launch | Keep gate rows marked `Gated? Owner`; PRs may document but not perform. |
146	
147	## 6. Honest limits
148	
149	A green docs-only PR proves only that the project is tracked and links are valid. It does not fix runtime debt.
150	
151	A green unit suite does not prove GPU, WSL, real-video, Cloudflare, Google Drive desktop, or GCS bucket behavior unless the PR explicitly runs those paths or records owner verification.
152	
153	Screencasts prove rendered behavior for the captured viewport and flow; they do not replace automated tests. Any UI PR that fixes a bug must still add or update deterministic tests where feasible.
154

199	## 9. Definition of done
200	
201	- [ ] All non-owner-gated P0/P1/P2/P3 audit findings either resolved, explicitly reclassified, or moved into a narrower tracked project.
202	- [ ] Every code repo has a credible local/CI quality gate for its size: tests, lint, type checking where appropriate, and coverage floor where feasible.
203	- [ ] Engine cost leaks and scratch leaks are fixed or guarded.
204	- [ ] Engine vendored SPA bundle is current or has a durable staleness signal.
205	- [ ] SPA processing UX survives refresh and gives testers visible job/file state.
206	- [ ] UI-visible PRs include validated visual artifacts and deterministic tests where feasible.
207	- [ ] Stale deployment docs/issues no longer direct future sessions at retired infrastructure.
208	- [ ] Owner-gated items remain clearly labeled and have no accidental code/data mutation.
209	- [ ] This doc's section 7 rows are updated with merged PR numbers.
210	- [ ] `Status` flips to `DONE`, inbound references are updated, and the doc is archived per `DOC_STATUS.md` section 2.

**#390 is out of scope** (owner excluded it) and left open; it is tracked as P23 (stale step-1 `main.py` split — re-cut fresh from `origin/master`).

### Execution design — wave model

Each wave is a theme for scheduling; each tracker row is an independent PR. A later wave can begin only when it does not conflict with active uncommitted work in the same repo.

- **Wave 0 — tracking and truth sync:** the docs-only bootstrap PR (#423) + this consolidation.
- **Wave 1 — engine cost/correctness:** paid re-bill, remote-source egress, ffmpeg timeouts, failed mypy fix. ✅ complete (P1–P4).
- **Wave 2 — engine resource durability:** temp scratch lifecycle, GDrive API streaming/safe put, Cloud Run timeout handling, upload retention scheduling. (P5–P7 done; P8 open.)
- **Wave 3 — supply-chain and CI gates:** SHA pins, lock/constraints plan, obsreport CI, sports-data-collector ruff/coverage, khelsutra E2E CUJ, workflow dedupe.
- **Wave 4 — UX/UI resilience:** vendored SPA bundle, job resume/progress, upload filename, demo-grid hiding, HTML 5xx handling, WhatsApp file share. This wave carries the screencast requirement.
- **Wave 5 — structural debt:** close/re-cut `#390`, continue `main.py` split, then shim/cycle cleanup and database split only after behavior fixes settle. (Absorbs the prior audit's D1/P10.)
- **Wave 6 — issue hygiene and archival:** close/update stale issues, archive terminal docs only after due diligence.

### Verification policy

For every code PR:

- Run the repo's normal CI-equivalent checks locally when feasible, or cite the green CI rollup and run only local checks that CI cannot cover, matching the repo's review policy.
- Preserve or raise coverage floors. Never lower a coverage floor without owner approval.
- Install missing test dependencies if the suite otherwise fails to collect; record unavoidable private/GPU/real-video skips.
- For engine PRs, default target is `python run_all_tests.py --cov=backend --cov=training --cov-report=term --cov-fail-under=70`, `ruff check .`, and `mypy backend training`, adjusted only for documented CI-policy exceptions.
- For `khelsutra/web` PRs, run typecheck, coverage, build, and any relevant Playwright CUJ; create/validate screencast evidence for UI behavior.
- For visual/UI PRs, attach the screencast or screenshots in the PR body and store durable artifacts under the existing `khelsutra-screencast/` workflow when the repo's harness supports it.

### Risk table

| Risk | Where it bites | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| Shared checkout absorbs sibling changes | All repos | Fetch/branch from `origin/<base>`, re-verify branch + HEAD before staging, stage explicit paths. |
| Docs-only PR accidentally includes local WIP | Especially collector `.gitignore` | Docs PRs touch docs only; the collector PR stages explicit paths and handles `.gitignore` deliberately. |
| Engine behavior fixes conflict with stale `#390` | `backend/main.py` | Land correctness/resource fixes first, then close/re-cut the split from fresh `origin/master`. |
| UI fixes regress layout or localized text | `khelsutra/web`, `site`, extension UI | Unit/e2e coverage plus Playwright screenshots/screencast validation. |
| Local cleanup looks durable but is machine-only | untracked debris / `.git` gc | Record local-only as local-only; add durable ignore/cleanup tests only when needed. |
| Owner-gated work gets blended into engineering PRs | Cloudflare, corpus, legal, launch | Keep gate rows marked `Gated? Owner`; PRs may document but not perform. |

### Honest limits

A green docs-only PR proves only that the project is tracked and links are valid. It does not fix runtime debt.

A green unit suite does not prove GPU, WSL, real-video, Cloudflare, Google Drive desktop, or GCS bucket behavior unless the PR explicitly runs those paths or records owner verification.

Screencasts prove rendered behavior for the captured viewport and flow; they do not replace automated tests. Any UI PR that fixes a bug must still add or update deterministic tests where feasible.

### Open questions — owner / external

1. For `sports-data-collector/.gitignore`, should model artifacts ever live in that repo, or should the WIP hunk be discarded because artifacts belong only in the engine repo?
2. For Roora pilot backup, approve metadata/CVAT CSV archive to GCS now? The plan treats this as low-cost/non-gated, but it still writes to the corpus bucket.
3. For the six owned source videos, should Phase 2 upload full 4K sources or only proxies once `F:` is mounted and hashes are recorded?
4. For upload-retention scheduling, should the default hosted wrapper be time-window-only, or should it skip files referenced by non-terminal job rows?
5. For SPA/UI PR evidence, should durable screencasts be checked into docs/artifacts, or kept in the existing non-git `khelsutra-screencast/` artifact sink with PR links?

### Definition of done

- [ ] All non-owner-gated P0/P1/P2/P3 audit findings either resolved, explicitly reclassified, or moved into a narrower tracked project.
- [ ] Every code repo has a credible local/CI quality gate for its size: tests, lint, type checking where appropriate, and coverage floor where feasible.
- [x] Engine cost leaks and scratch leaks are fixed or guarded (P2–P7).
- [ ] Engine vendored SPA bundle is current or has a durable staleness signal.
- [ ] SPA processing UX survives refresh and gives testers visible job/file state.
- [ ] UI-visible PRs include validated visual artifacts and deterministic tests where feasible.
- [ ] Stale deployment docs/issues no longer direct future sessions at retired infrastructure.
- [ ] Owner-gated items remain clearly labeled and have no accidental code/data mutation.
- [x] This tracker's rows are kept current with merged PR numbers.
- [ ] When the waves complete, flip this umbrella `Status → DONE`, update inbound references, and archive per `DOC_STATUS.md` §2.

1	- [RALLY_QUALITY_RESEARCH.md](RALLY_QUALITY_RESEARCH.md) — **★ Foundation doc** for closing the local-vs-Gemini rally-detection gap: the measured gap + the code-grounded over-merge diagnosis ("shuttle moving ≠ rally"), an inventory of the eval/experiment harness + its gaps, the strengthened evaluation framework (segmental Edit/F1@k that make over-segmentation first-class, golden-corpus + active learning, Gemini-agreement as a *shadow* signal), an **adversarially-verified, cited external-research synthesis** (TAL/TAS boundary heads, racket-sports rally-state cues, small-data, eval rigor), a **prioritized cheap→durable roadmap with falsifiable success criteria**, and (§7) a **tracked project plan** — work-item checklist, sequencing, the single quality **number** attributed to the effort, and a **definition of done** (project completes when all tiers land + the number is recorded). The plan that the [QUALITY_ITERATIONS.md](QUALITY_ITERATIONS.md) log executes against; ties together EXPERIMENT_HARNESS / MULTI_SIGNAL_FUSION_PLAN / ANALYZERS_AND_RECONCILERS / OWNED_MODEL_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.
2	- [RALLY_DETECTION_GAP_CLOSURE.md](RALLY_DETECTION_GAP_CLOSURE.md) — **Tracked execution doc (2026-06-18)** for closing the remaining rally-detection accuracy gap **per video, before launch**, driven by the growing golden corpus. First ground-truth clip (GX010141) reframed it: boundaries are tight (R2/R3/R4 holds) — the gap is **detection**: (G1) recall (local & Gemini both miss ~half on hard clips, and *different* rallies → complementary), (G2) precision (local over-fires), (G3) **rally-END over-extension on the quick-return-after-point** (shuttle still moving → needs a *player-state* signal). Levers (owner-gated): player-kinematics + shuttle-in-hand / sustained-invisibility end-rule with reverse-timeline backtrack, `rally_gate` Gate A wiring, complementary fusion. **Peer** of `RALLY_QUALITY_RESEARCH.md` (execution tracker, not research); §7 is the source of truth.
3	- [PROMINENT_COURT_DETECTION.md](PROMINENT_COURT_DETECTION.md) — **Design + experiment (2026-06-19, default-OFF, owner-gated flip)** for the **multicourt G2 over-fire**: identify the **prominent (foreground) court** (the one covering the majority of the frame) and keep only rallies whose shuttle lives inside it — re-defining the shuttle signal `(x,y,visible)` → `(x,y,visible-AND-inside-prominent-court)` *before* windowing, so a background/adjacent-court shuttle never forms a rally. 2D floor polygon (MVP) → **pseudo-3D play-volume** (refinement, so high clears aren't clipped). Born from the GX010142 rally-#1 background-court FP; reuses `fusion_features.point_in_polygon` + `FusionConfig.court_polygon`. §8 progress tracker is the source of truth.
4	- [RALLY_DETECTION_QUALITY_REPORT.md](RALLY_DETECTION_QUALITY_REPORT.md) — **Technical-paper checkpoint (2026-06-17)** of rally-detection quality: the honest measured state (over-segmentation milestone tolerance-F1 0.091→0.323 at n=11, p=0.002; merge-rate 0.694→0.150; first real-footage ground-truth at-par with START at Gemini parity), the task-faithful R2 metric, a detailed Gemini comparison, next steps + expected wins, areas yet to be explored, and a publishability / path-to-submission verdict. Renders to a polished PDF; the externally-shareable "toward a paper" summary of `RALLY_QUALITY_RESEARCH.md` + `QUALITY_ITERATIONS.md`.

6	- [EXPERIMENT_HARNESS.md](EXPERIMENT_HARNESS.md) — Design (P1 SHIPPED) for A/B + shadow + SxS experimentation on rally-detection variants with **zero production regression** — experiments are decoupled from deploy (new cues default OFF, promote only on measured LOVO lift, rollback = flip a flag). Composes the existing `backend/eval/` machinery (`calibration`, `regression`, `metrics`); implemented in `backend/eval/experiment.py`.
7	- [QUALITY_ITERATIONS.md](QUALITY_ITERATIONS.md) — **Living log** of rally-detection quality iterations (hypothesis → what shipped → measured/expected effect → lesson). The reverse-chronological record; terminal-state snapshots get archived under `archives/quality-iterations/`.
8	- [ANALYZERS_AND_RECONCILERS.md](ANALYZERS_AND_RECONCILERS.md) — Design (owner-gated, NOT started): a **signal-fusion framework** for rally detection — producers emit confidence-scored **signals** at three temporal **scopes** (frame detectors / window analyzers / session analyzers), a learned **scorer** weights them, and an auditable **report-first reconciliation** pass lints the decision against the evidence. The spine: **no special-casing in the decision path** (signals→scorer, never `if/else`). Worked examples: a service-fault analyzer, a warm-up/practice span detector, and consuming a per-frame shuttle-state detector. A peer to `EXPERIMENT_HARNESS.md` and `MULTI_SIGNAL_FUSION_PLAN.md` (ADR-007 modular rally layer).
9	- [PERSONALIZATION_PLAN.md](PERSONALIZATION_PLAN.md) — Design (owner-adopted **hybrid**; Phase-0 landing) for the **per-user personalization feature on a generalization-first baseline**: a thin per-user *tuning* layer gated by the honest small-N statistics (a **min-N promotion floor** + a **structural-guard exemption**, never a service gate) + a **contribution flywheel** (free-tier videos pool into the owner-trained baseline; *"a tool that gets better the more you help it"*). Records the **locked owner decisions** (identity, storage, `min_n` semantics, Gate-A default, free-tier pooling). Built so far: per-user promotion gate (#141) + held-out-USER learning curve (#142).

=== #390 body (design intent) ===
## Summary

First step of the **P10 / D1** item from `docs/CODE_CLEANUP_AUDIT_2026-06-24.md`: split the `backend/main.py` god-module. This PR extracts the **processing + compile orchestration** into a new `backend/orchestration.py`, reducing `main.py` from **1797 → 1217 lines** (32% reduction).

> **Scope note:** This is the first of a multi-step split (P10a). The CLI (`main()`) and route handlers stay in `main.py` for now — they're tightly coupled to the module globals (`global_config`/`db_instance`) and the `app` object, so moving them needs careful global-mutation handling (tracked in #388 / P10b). This PR proves the extraction pattern and delivers the biggest single win.

## What moved → `backend/orchestration.py` (635 lines)

| Function | Purpose |
|----------|---------|
| `_finalize_reingest` | Destroy-AFTER-confirm segment commit |
| `_ingest_remote_segments` | Cloud job result → DB rows |
| `_cloud_available` | Can this box dispatch a cloud job? |
| `_maybe_dispatch_remote` | Cloud Run dispatch + ingest (default-OFF) |
| `_execute_processing` | Full hash → validate → segment → report pipeline |
| `_CompileError` | Submit-time-validatable compile problem |
| `_resolve_compile` | Shared submit/worker compile resolution |
| `_execute_compile` | Worker handler for a compile job |

## Proof this is behavior-preserving

AST-verified (not just "looks safe"):
- ✅ All 8 extracted functions are **AST-identical** to the originals in `main.py` (except 2 intentional, semantically-neutral changes to avoid a circular import — see below).
- ✅ All **30 retained functions** in `main.py` (routes + CLI + helpers) are **byte-identical to master**.
- ✅ The **21 consumers** that do `import backend.main as m` and access `_execute_processing` / `_resolve_compile` / etc. keep working unchanged — the symbols are re-exported via the import.

### The 2 intentional AST deltas (circular-import avoidance)
1. `_maybe_dispatch_remote`: the `ProcessRequest` annotation is now a string forward-ref (`"ProcessRequest"`) instead of a bare `Name` — avoids importing `backend.main` at module load.
2. `_execute_processing`: the backward-compat shim that reads `db_instance`/`global_config` now imports them **lazily** from `backend.main` inside the function body (was a bare module-global reference) — defers the circular import to call-time.

Both are annotation/import-mechanics only; runtime behavior is identical.

## Follow-ups (tracked)
- **#388** — migrate the async-job plumbing (`_get_executor`/`_drain_due_jobs`/`_arm_wake_timer`) out of `main.py`.
- **P10b** — extract the CLI (`main()`) and route handlers, handling the `global` statement + `app` coupling.

=== create_app body (how it relates to module-level app) ===
from backend.utils.redact import (
    redact_secrets,  # P0b: mask secret-shaped fields in /api/config
)
from backend.utils.single_instance import acquire_lock  # P0e: one instance per database

app = FastAPI(title="Sports Highlight Indexer API")


def create_app(config: AppConfig, db: Database) -> FastAPI:
    """Factory: inject shared state via ``app.state`` so route handlers access it through
    ``request.app.state`` — eliminating the Optional module-level globals that forced
    ``backend.main`` and ``backend.api.job_worker`` into mypy quarantine.

    Also sets the module-level ``global_config`` / ``db_instance`` for transitional
    compatibility (helpers and tests still reference them). Route handlers use
    ``request.app.state`` exclusively."""
    global global_config, db_instance
    global_config = config
    db_instance = db
    app.state.config = config
    app.state.db = db
    return app

### 244. assistant (2026-07-02T09:06:29.445Z)

Closing to **re-cut fresh from `origin/master`** (owner-approved; tracked as umbrella Remediation-tracker **P23**).

The design here was right — extract the processing/compile orchestration with AST-identity proof and a `backend.main` re-export shim — and the re-cut reuses that pattern. But this PR can no longer be taken over:

- It was cut when `main.py` was **1797 lines**; master's `main.py` is now **2360** (~10 PRs landed in it since 2026-06-26), so the PR is CONFLICTING/DIRTY.
- More importantly, the **extracted copies are stale implementations**: since this branch was cut, `_execute_processing` gained content-hash idempotency + `force` persistence (#424) and scratch-dir cleanup (#427), `/api/source`-adjacent orchestration changed (#425), `_execute_compile`/compile paths gained scratch cleanup (#427) and ffmpeg timeout mapping (#426), and `_maybe_dispatch_remote`'s Cloud Run path gained rescue-or-cancel salvage (#417). Merging (or mechanically rebasing) this extraction would risk resurrecting pre-fix behavior in the moved bodies — exactly the wrong risk profile for the heart of the system.

**Re-cut plan (started):** same target module + same seam discipline as the `backend.api.job_worker` extraction — every cross-function seam resolved through the `backend.main` module object at call time, names re-exported from `backend.main` so all existing `monkeypatch.setattr(backend.main, …)` seams and `import backend.main as m` consumers keep working — but extracting **today's** function bodies, with AST-identity verification against current master, plus a **line-budget ratchet test** so `main.py` cannot silently regrow.

=== __main__ block (module-identity trap) ===
if __name__ == "__main__":
    # `python -m backend.main` runs THIS file as `__main__`, but `job_worker`/`uploads` resolve the
    # shared mutable state (`db_instance`, `global_config`) via `import backend.main` — a SEPARATE
    # module object. If `main()` runs here it configures `__main__`'s globals, leaving
    # `backend.main.db_instance` = None; the async-job drain then crashes silently on
    # `None.claim_due_job()` and every submitted job hangs in 'queued' forever. Dispatch through
    # `backend.main` so `main()` configures the module everyone actually reads. (No recursion:
    # imported as `backend.main`, `__name__` != "__main__", so this block doesn't re-fire.)
    import backend.main as _bm

    _bm.main()

# modules and pins an explicit quarantine list below — not a typing crusade.
# Ratchet: when you touch a quarantined module, try removing its entry.
ignore_missing_imports = True
# backend/ is a namespace package (no root __init__.py) — required so mypy
# maps files to backend.* module names instead of duplicating top-level names.
explicit_package_bases = True

# --- Quarantine (known-loose modules, each with a reason) ---

# Route handlers use request.app.state exclusively; helpers take explicit params.
# backend.api.job_worker lifted from quarantine (now takes explicit config/db params).

ignore_errors = True

# at runtime (the .get() compat layer). Shares backend.main's config-shim quarantine

ignore_errors = True

368	def _finalize_reingest(db, video_id: str, segments, play_wm: int, cand_wm: int) -> None:
369	    """Commit a (re)segmentation with destroy-AFTER-confirm semantics.
370	
371	    On a non-empty run, drop the PRIOR rows (id <= the pre-run watermark) so only the
372	    fresh segments remain. On an empty run, drop the NEW partial rows (id > watermark) and
373	    keep the prior good results intact — a failed/empty re-run never destroys prior data.
374	    """
375	    if segments:
376	        db.prune_segments(video_id, play_upto=play_wm, cand_upto=cand_wm)
377	    else:
378	        db.prune_segments(video_id, play_after=play_wm, cand_after=cand_wm)

976	def _ingest_remote_segments(
977	    db, video_id: str, outputs_uri: str, storage_config: dict, *, max_rows: int = 20000
978	) -> int:
979	    """Read a cloud job's ``intervals.csv`` (start,end,shot_count,ending_reason) from the bucket and
980	    persist each rally via the SAME ``db.add_play_segment`` the in-process segmenters call — so
981	    ``/api/query`` + ``/api/compile`` read the rows with zero knowledge of where they were produced.
982	    Returns the count ingested. Mirrors ``cloud_run._read_json``'s fetch-to-a-real-dst pattern (cloud
983	    backends REQUIRE a dst). Hardened against a hostile/corrupt worker output: a malformed row is
984	    skipped, non-finite / overflowing values are rejected, the loop is bounded, and the temp dir is
985	    cleaned. May raise (missing/unreadable CSV, fetch error) — the caller treats that as a job failure
986	    and prunes any partial rows (destroy-AFTER-confirm)."""
987	    uri = f"{outputs_uri.rstrip('/')}/intervals.csv"
988	    ref = storage.parse_uri(uri)
989	    n = 0
990	    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="cr-ingest-") as td:
991	        local = storage.fetch(
992	            uri, os.path.join(td, ref.name or "intervals.csv"), config=storage_config
993	        )
994	        with open(local, newline="", encoding="utf-8") as f:
995	            for row in csv.DictReader(f):
996	                if n >= max_rows:
997	                    logger.warning(
998	                        "[API] cloud ingest hit the %d-row cap (%s) — ignoring the rest.",
999	                        max_rows,
1000	                        uri,
1001	                    )
1002	                    break
1003	                try:
1004	                    start, end = float(row["start"]), float(row["end"])
1005	                except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError):
1006	                    continue
1007	                if not (math.isfinite(start) and math.isfinite(end)):
1008	                    continue  # reject inf/nan times rather than persist a junk rally
1009	                meta: Dict[str, Any] = {
1010	                    "source": "cloud_run",
1011	                    "ending_reason": (row.get("ending_reason") or "").strip(),
1012	                }
1013	                sc = (row.get("shot_count") or "").strip()
1014	                if sc:
1015	                    try:
1016	                        meta["shot_count"] = int(
1017	                            float(sc)
1018	                        )  # OverflowError on 'inf'/'1e400'
1019	                    except (ValueError, OverflowError):
1020	                        meta["shot_count"] = sc
1021	                db.add_play_segment(video_id, start, end, metadata=meta)
1022	                n += 1
1023	    return n

1026	def _cloud_available(cfg) -> bool:
1027	    """Whether THIS server can satisfy a per-request ``compute="cloud"`` dispatch (item 3).
1028	
1029	    True when the control plane is wired for Cloud Run: either the configured default target is
1030	    already ``cloud_run`` (the operator chose cloud serving), or the Cloud Run env coordinates
1031	    (``CLOUD_RUN_JOB`` + ``GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT``) and an output bucket (``storage.output_root``)
1032	    are present so a forced per-request override can actually dispatch + collect a result.
1033	
1034	    Surfaced in ``/api/capabilities`` so the SPA only offers the cloud toggle when it would work."""
1035	    deployment = getattr(cfg, "deployment", None)
1036	    if (getattr(deployment, "compute_target", "") or "") == "cloud_run":
1037	        return True
1038	    has_job = bool(
1039	        os.environ.get("CLOUD_RUN_JOB") and os.environ.get("GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT")
1040	    )
1041	    out_root = (getattr(getattr(cfg, "storage", None), "output_root", "") or "").strip()
1042	    return bool(has_job and out_root)

1062	    doesn't claim a fake segmentation run) — or ``None`` to fall through to the in-process segmenter
1063	    (**DEFAULT-OFF**: ``get_compute_backend`` returns ``None`` for every non-``cloud_run`` target, so

1067	    def _failed(msg: str, ran: bool) -> Tuple[Dict[str, Any], bool]:
1068	        # Shape-match the in-process segmenter-fail branch + drop any partial NEW rows (id > play_wm);
1069	        # prior good rows (id <= play_wm) are preserved (destroy-AFTER-confirm).
1070	        db.prune_segments(video_id, play_after=play_wm, cand_after=cand_wm)
1071	        # Provenance even on failure, and HONEST about what ran: `path` is "cloud_run" only when the
1072	        # job actually dispatched + executed remotely (ran=True, e.g. a non-zero worker rc / ingest
1073	        # error); a pre-dispatch reject (ran=False) ran NOWHERE → "not_run". `target` records the
1074	        # intended backend, `requested` the picker choice, `dispatched` whether the remote job ran.
1075	        return (
1076	            {
1077	                "video_id": video_id,
1078	                "status": "failed",
1079	                "message": msg,
1080	                "results": [r.model_dump() for r in val_results],
1081	                "play_segments": [],
1082	                "compute": {
1083	                    "path": "cloud_run" if ran else "not_run",
1084	                    "requested": req.compute,
1085	                    "target": "cloud_run",
1086	                    "dispatched": ran,
1087	                },
1088	            },
1089	            ran,
1090	        )
1091	
1092	    # SPA compute-picker (item 3): a per-request choice overrides the server's configured default.
1093	    # "local" forces the in-process segmenter; "cloud" forces a Cloud Run dispatch (guarded by
1094	    # _cloud_available so we fail clearly rather than silently running local); None / unknown → default.
1095	    choice = (req.compute or "").strip().lower()
1096	    if choice and choice not in ("local", "cloud"):
1097	        logger.warning(
1098	            "[API] ignoring unknown compute=%r (expected 'local'|'cloud'); using server default.",
1099	            req.compute,
1100	        )
1101	        choice = ""
1102	    if choice == "local":
1103	        return None  # explicit "run on my machine" — in-process regardless of the server's target
1104	    if choice == "cloud":
1105	        if not _cloud_available(cfg):
1106	            return _failed(

1479	def _remote_input_size_bytes(input_ref, storage_config) -> Optional[int]:
1480	    """Best-effort size (bytes) of a remote storage source, or ``None`` if unknowable.
1481	
1482	    Used only to pre-reserve scratch for a fetch-to-local (P2). Never raises: a stat that needs a
1483	    missing cloud SDK, the network, or a credential returns ``None`` so the free-space guard degrades
1484	    to a no-op rather than blocking a job it cannot measure."""
1485	    try:
1486	        sc = (
1487	            storage_config.model_dump()
1488	            if hasattr(storage_config, "model_dump")
1489	            else (storage_config or {})
1490	        )
1491	        backend = storage.get_storage(input_ref.backend, config=sc)
1492	        return int(backend.stat(input_ref).size)
1493	    except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 - measuring is best-effort; inability must not block
1494	        logger.debug(
1495	            "free-space: could not stat remote source %s: %s", input_ref.uri, e
1496	        )
1497	        return None
1498

1681	class _CompileError(Exception):
1682	    """A submit-time-validatable compile problem — carries the HTTP status + detail so the API path
1683	    maps it to an HTTPException (fast 4xx) and the worker path maps it to a failed-job result."""
1684	
1685	    def __init__(self, status_code: int, detail: str):
1686	        super().__init__(detail)
1687	        self.status_code = status_code
1688	        self.detail = detail
1689

1691	def _resolve_compile(
1692	    db, cfg, video_id: str, segment_ids: List[int], output_filename: str
1693	):
1694	    """Shared compile resolution — run at SUBMIT (fast-fail 4xx) AND in the WORKER (re-resolve, since a
1695	    row could change between submit and run). Verifies the video exists, the requested segments match
1696	    and survive the ``stitching.min_shot_count`` floor (segments WITHOUT a known shot_count are exempt
1697	    so manual/legacy picks can't silently vanish), and the output name is traversal-safe. Raises
1698	    ``_CompileError(404/400)``. Returns ``(video_row, kept_segments, safe_out_name)``."""
1699	    video = db.get_video(video_id)
1700	    if not video:
1701	        raise _CompileError(404, "Indexed video record not found.")
1702	    # Reject path traversal / absolute names (os.path.join would otherwise write anywhere on disk).
1703	    try:
1704	        out_name = safe_output_filename(output_filename)
1705	    except ValueError as e:
1706	        raise _CompileError(400, str(e)) from e
1707	
1708	    all_segments = db.query_play_segments(video_id, {})
1709	    matched = [s for s in all_segments if s["id"] in segment_ids]
1710	    if not matched:
1711	        raise _CompileError(400, "No matching play segments found to stitch.")
1712	
1713	    stitching = getattr(cfg, "stitching", None) or StitchingConfig()
1714	    kept = []
1715	    for s in matched:
1716	        meta = s.get("metadata") or {}
1717	        sc = meta.get("shot_count") if isinstance(meta, dict) else None
1718	        try:
1719	            if sc is not None and int(sc) < stitching.min_shot_count:
1720	                continue
1721	        except (TypeError, ValueError):
1722	            pass  # unparseable count -> treat as unknown, keep
1723	        kept.append(s)
1724	    if len(kept) < len(matched):
1725	        logger.info(
1726	            "[compile] stitching.min_shot_count=%s: dropped %d/%d segments below the floor.",
1727	            stitching.min_shot_count,
1728	            len(matched) - len(kept),
1729	            len(matched),
1730	        )
1731	    if not kept:
1732	        raise _CompileError(
1733	            400,
1734	            f"All {len(matched)} matching segments fall below "
1735	            f"stitching.min_shot_count={stitching.min_shot_count}.",
1736	        )
1737	    return video, kept, out_name

1741	def compile_highlights(req: CompileRequest, request: Request):
1742	    """Submit a reel-COMPILE job (202 + job_id); poll GET /api/jobs/{job_id} for the reel (#329).
1743	
1744	    Async, mirroring /api/process: the ffmpeg stitch (trim + re-encode each clip + concat + watermark)
1745	    can run minutes on CPU — past the ~100 s edge/proxy timeout — so doing it synchronously returned a
1746	    Cloudflare 524 while the engine kept going (the reel built, but the client saw "failed" and never
1747	    got the URL). The cheap checks (video exists, segments match + survive the floor, safe filename)
1748	    fast-fail HERE with a 4xx; the stitch runs on the worker. On `done`, the job's `result` carries
1749	    {filepath, download_url} — the same shape the sync endpoint returned, now under /api/jobs/{id}."""
1750	    # M9 edge-auth tenant (DEFAULT-OFF → None); a missing/spoofed identity fails 401 before queuing.
1751	    try:
1752	        owner_id = edge_auth.resolve_tenant(request.headers, request.app.state.config)
1753	    except edge_auth.EdgeAuthError as e:
1754	        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail=f"edge auth: {e}") from e
1755	    try:
1756	        video, _kept, out_name = _resolve_compile(
1757	            request.app.state.db,
1758	            request.app.state.config,
1759	            req.video_id,
1760	            req.segment_ids,
1761	            req.output_filename,
1762	        )
1763	    except _CompileError as e:
1764	        raise HTTPException(status_code=e.status_code, detail=e.detail) from e
1765	
1766	    job_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
1767	    # Persist the video's SOURCE ref (the NOT NULL video_path); the worker re-fetches the row by id so
1768	    # the freshest filepath wins. owner_id/locale ride along for M9 multi-tenant + the localized reel.
1769	    if not request.app.state.db.create_compile_job(
1770	        job_id,
1771	        req.video_id,
1772	        video["filepath"],
1773	        req.segment_ids,
1774	        out_name,
1775	        locale=req.locale,
1776	        owner_id=owner_id,
1777	    ):
1778	        raise HTTPException(
1779	            status_code=500, detail="Failed to persist compile job record."
1780	        )
1781	    _get_executor().submit(
1782	        _drain_due_jobs, request.app.state.config, request.app.state.db
1783	    )
1784	    return JSONResponse(
1785	        status_code=202,
1786	        content={"job_id": job_id, "status": "queued", "poll": f"/api/jobs/{job_id}"},
1787	    )

1790	def _execute_compile(cfg, db, job: Dict[str, Any], progress=None) -> Dict[str, Any]:
1791	    """Worker handler for a ``kind='compile'`` job (#329): re-resolve the segments, stitch the reel,
1792	    and return the {status, filepath, download_url} result the SPA polls for. Returns a
1793	    ``status='failed'`` result for an EXPECTED problem (missing video / no segments / a stitch error)
1794	    so the SPA always gets a clean verdict instead of a worker-crash error."""

1798	    segment_ids = params.get("segment_ids") or []
1799	    locale = params.get("locale")
1800

1803	        video, kept, out_name = _resolve_compile(

1805	        )
1806	    except _CompileError as e:
1807	        return {"status": "failed", "message": e.detail, "video_id": video_id}

def _report_config_for_input(config: Any, raw_input: str) -> Any:
    """Return report config that keeps remote-input reports out of disposable scratch."""
    storage_config = (
        config.get("storage") if isinstance(config, dict) else getattr(config, "storage", None)
    )
    try:
        if storage.input_is_local(raw_input, storage_config):
            return config
    except ValueError:
        return config
    try:
        cfg = (
            config.model_dump(mode="json")
            if hasattr(config, "model_dump")
            else dict(config or {})
        )
    except Exception:
        cfg = {}
    report = dict(cfg.get("report") or {})
    if report.get("output_dir"):
        return config
    report["output_dir"] = os.path.join(output_base(config), "reports")
    cfg["report"] = report
    return cfg

"""Scripted extraction of the orchestration block from backend/main.py (P23 re-cut of #390).

import io
import sys
